codex
The benchmark is a single-process CSR implementation. I’m restructuring it so MPI partitions rows across ranks, each rank offloads its local CSR slice to a CUDA GPU, OpenMP handles host-side parallel work, and rank 0 preserves the original CLI/output and validation semantics.

+static void spmvCpu(const double* val, const index_t* col, const index_t* row,
+                    const double* x, index_t nrows, double* y) {
+#pragma omp parallel for schedule(dynamic, 64)
+    for (int64_t i = 0; i < static_cast<int64_t>(nrows); ++i) {
+        double sum = 0.0;
+        for (index_t j = row[i]; j < row[i + 1]; ++j) sum += val[j] * x[col[j]];
+        y[i] = sum;
     }
 }

-// ****************************************************************************
-bool verifyResults(const double* reference, const double* result, const index_t size) {
-    for (index_t i = 0; i < size; ++i) {

-        const double res = result[i];
-        if (std::abs(ref) < 1e-10) {

-            if (std::abs(res) > MAX_RELATIVE_ERROR) {

-                return false;

-            const double relError = std::abs((res - ref) / ref);

-                       i, ref, res, relError);
-                return false;

+// A warp owns a row.  This is substantially better than one thread per row for
+// the benchmark's long rows and remains efficient for short, irregular rows.
+__global__ void spmvWarpKernel(const double* __restrict__ val,
+                               const index_t* __restrict__ col,
+                               const index_t* __restrict__ row,
+                               const double* __restrict__ x,
+                               index_t nrows, double* __restrict__ y) {
+    const unsigned lane = threadIdx.x & 31u;
+    const index_t r = (blockIdx.x * blockDim.x + threadIdx.x) >> 5;
+    if (r >= nrows) return;
+    double sum = 0.0;
+    for (index_t j = row[r] + lane; j < row[r + 1]; j += 32)
+        sum += val[j] * __ldg(x + col[j]);
+    for (int offset = 16; offset; offset >>= 1)
+        sum += __shfl_down_sync(0xffffffffu, sum, offset);
+    if (lane == 0) y[r] = sum;
+}
+
+static bool verifyResults(const double* ref, const double* got, index_t n) {
+    for (index_t i = 0; i < n; ++i) {
+        const double err = std::abs(got[i] - ref[i]);
+        if ((std::abs(ref[i]) < 1e-10 && err > MAX_RELATIVE_ERROR) ||
+            (std::abs(ref[i]) >= 1e-10 && err / std::abs(ref[i]) > MAX_RELATIVE_ERROR)) {
+            std::printf("Validation failed at index %u: reference %.10e, got %.10e\n",
+                        i, ref[i], got[i]);
+            return false;
         }
     }
     return true;
 }

+static void spmvCpu(const double* val, const index_t* col, const index_t* row,
+                    const double* x, index_t nrows, double* y) {
+#pragma omp parallel for schedule(dynamic, 64)
+    for (int64_t i = 0; i < static_cast<int64_t>(nrows); ++i) {
+        double sum = 0.0;
+        for (index_t j = row[i]; j < row[i + 1]; ++j) sum += val[j] * x[col[j]];
+        y[i] = sum;
     }
 }

-                       i, ref, res, relError);
-                return false;
-            }
+// A warp owns a row.  This is substantially better than one thread per row for
+// the benchmark's long rows and remains efficient for short, irregular rows.
+__global__ void spmvWarpKernel(const double* __restrict__ val,
+                               const index_t* __restrict__ col,
+                               const index_t* __restrict__ row,
+                               const double* __restrict__ x,
+                               index_t nrows, double* __restrict__ y) {
+    const unsigned lane = threadIdx.x & 31u;
+    const index_t r = (blockIdx.x * blockDim.x + threadIdx.x) >> 5;
+    if (r >= nrows) return;
+    double sum = 0.0;
+    for (index_t j = row[r] + lane; j < row[r + 1]; j += 32)
+        sum += val[j] * __ldg(x + col[j]);
+    for (int offset = 16; offset; offset >>= 1)
+        sum += __shfl_down_sync(0xffffffffu, sum, offset);
+    if (lane == 0) y[r] = sum;
+}
+
+static bool verifyResults(const double* ref, const double* got, index_t n) {
+    for (index_t i = 0; i < n; ++i) {
+        const double err = std::abs(got[i] - ref[i]);
+        if ((std::abs(ref[i]) < 1e-10 && err > MAX_RELATIVE_ERROR) ||
+            (std::abs(ref[i]) >= 1e-10 && err / std::abs(ref[i]) > MAX_RELATIVE_ERROR)) {
+            std::printf("Validation failed at index %u: reference %.10e, got %.10e\n",
+                        i, ref[i], got[i]);
+            return false;
         }
     }
     return true;
 }

codex
The hybrid path is now wired end to end, including GPU selection by node-local MPI rank and persistent device allocations outside the timed loop. I’m compiling it now and will correct any CUDA/MPI toolchain issues before running validation.

+static void spmvCpu(const double* val, const index_t* col, const index_t* row,
+                    const double* x, index_t nrows, double* y) {
+#pragma omp parallel for schedule(dynamic, 64)
+    for (int64_t i = 0; i < static_cast<int64_t>(nrows); ++i) {
+        double sum = 0.0;
+        for (index_t j = row[i]; j < row[i + 1]; ++j) sum += val[j] * x[col[j]];
+        y[i] = sum;
     }
 }

+// A warp owns a row.  This is substantially better than one thread per row for
+// the benchmark's long rows and remains efficient for short, irregular rows.
+__global__ void spmvWarpKernel(const double* __restrict__ val,
+                               const index_t* __restrict__ col,
+                               const index_t* __restrict__ row,
+                               const double* __restrict__ x,
+                               index_t nrows, double* __restrict__ y) {
+    const unsigned lane = threadIdx.x & 31u;
+    const index_t r = (blockIdx.x * blockDim.x + threadIdx.x) >> 5;
+    if (r >= nrows) return;
+    double sum = 0.0;
+    for (index_t j = row[r] + lane; j < row[r + 1]; j += 32)
+        sum += val[j] * __ldg(x + col[j]);
+    for (int offset = 16; offset; offset >>= 1)
+        sum += __shfl_down_sync(0xffffffffu, sum, offset);
+    if (lane == 0) y[r] = sum;
+}
+
+static bool verifyResults(const double* ref, const double* got, index_t n) {
+    for (index_t i = 0; i < n; ++i) {
+        const double err = std::abs(got[i] - ref[i]);
+        if ((std::abs(ref[i]) < 1e-10 && err > MAX_RELATIVE_ERROR) ||
+            (std::abs(ref[i]) >= 1e-10 && err / std::abs(ref[i]) > MAX_RELATIVE_ERROR)) {
+            std::printf("Validation failed at index %u: reference %.10e, got %.10e\n",
+                        i, ref[i], got[i]);
+            return false;
         }
     }
     return true;
 }

+    const double elapsed = MPI_Wtime() - start;

+        const double ms = global_elapsed * 1000.0;
+        std::printf("Computation time: %.3f ms\nAverage time per iteration: %.3f ms\nPerformance: %.3f GFLOPS\n",
+                    ms, ms / iterations, 2.0 * nnz * iterations / global_elapsed / 1e9);

+        if (validate) {
+            std::printf("Validating result...\n");

     }

+    MPI_Finalize();

+static void spmvCpu(const double* val, const index_t* col, const index_t* row,
+                    const double* x, index_t nrows, double* y) {
+#pragma omp parallel for schedule(dynamic, 64)
+    for (int64_t i = 0; i < static_cast<int64_t>(nrows); ++i) {
+        double sum = 0.0;
+        for (index_t j = row[i]; j < row[i + 1]; ++j) sum += val[j] * x[col[j]];
+        y[i] = sum;
     }

+// A warp owns a row.  This is substantially better than one thread per row for
+// the benchmark's long rows and remains efficient for short, irregular rows.
+__global__ void spmvWarpKernel(const double* __restrict__ val,
+                               const index_t* __restrict__ col,
+                               const index_t* __restrict__ row,
+                               const double* __restrict__ x,
+                               index_t nrows, double* __restrict__ y) {
+    const unsigned lane = threadIdx.x & 31u;
+    const index_t r = (blockIdx.x * blockDim.x + threadIdx.x) >> 5;
+    if (r >= nrows) return;
+    double sum = 0.0;
+    for (index_t j = row[r] + lane; j < row[r + 1]; j += 32)
+        sum += val[j] * __ldg(x + col[j]);
+    for (int offset = 16; offset; offset >>= 1)
+        sum += __shfl_down_sync(0xffffffffu, sum, offset);
+    if (lane == 0) y[r] = sum;
+}
+
+static bool verifyResults(const double* ref, const double* got, index_t n) {
+    for (index_t i = 0; i < n; ++i) {
+        const double err = std::abs(got[i] - ref[i]);
+        if ((std::abs(ref[i]) < 1e-10 && err > MAX_RELATIVE_ERROR) ||
+            (std::abs(ref[i]) >= 1e-10 && err / std::abs(ref[i]) > MAX_RELATIVE_ERROR)) {
+            std::printf("Validation failed at index %u: reference %.10e, got %.10e\n",
+                        i, ref[i], got[i]);
+            return false;
         }
     }
     return true;
 }

+static void spmvCpu(const double* val, const index_t* col, const index_t* row,
+                    const double* x, index_t nrows, double* y) {
+#pragma omp parallel for schedule(dynamic, 64)
+    for (int64_t i = 0; i < static_cast<int64_t>(nrows); ++i) {
+        double sum = 0.0;

+// A warp owns a row.  This is substantially better than one thread per row for
+// the benchmark's long rows and remains efficient for short, irregular rows.
+__global__ void spmvWarpKernel(const double* __restrict__ val,
+                               const index_t* __restrict__ col,
+                               const index_t* __restrict__ row,
+                               const double* __restrict__ x,
+                               index_t nrows, double* __restrict__ y) {
+    const unsigned lane = threadIdx.x & 31u;
+    const index_t r = (blockIdx.x * blockDim.x + threadIdx.x) >> 5;
+    if (r >= nrows) return;
+    double sum = 0.0;
+    for (index_t j = row[r] + lane; j < row[r + 1]; j += 32)
+        sum += val[j] * __ldg(x + col[j]);
+    for (int offset = 16; offset; offset >>= 1)
+        sum += __shfl_down_sync(0xffffffffu, sum, offset);
+    if (lane == 0) y[r] = sum;
+}
+
+static bool verifyResults(const double* ref, const double* got, index_t n) {
+    for (index_t i = 0; i < n; ++i) {
+        const double err = std::abs(got[i] - ref[i]);
+        if ((std::abs(ref[i]) < 1e-10 && err > MAX_RELATIVE_ERROR) ||
+            (std::abs(ref[i]) >= 1e-10 && err / std::abs(ref[i]) > MAX_RELATIVE_ERROR)) {
+            std::printf("Validation failed at index %u: reference %.10e, got %.10e\n",
+                        i, ref[i], got[i]);
+            return false;
         }
     }
     return true;
 }

+static void spmvCpu(const double* val, const index_t* col, const index_t* row,
+                    const double* x, index_t nrows, double* y) {

+// A warp owns a row.  This is substantially better than one thread per row for
+// the benchmark's long rows and remains efficient for short, irregular rows.
+__global__ void spmvWarpKernel(const double* __restrict__ val,
+                               const index_t* __restrict__ col,
+                               const index_t* __restrict__ row,
+                               const double* __restrict__ x,
+                               index_t nrows, double* __restrict__ y) {
+    const unsigned lane = threadIdx.x & 31u;
+    const index_t r = (blockIdx.x * blockDim.x + threadIdx.x) >> 5;
+    if (r >= nrows) return;
+    double sum = 0.0;
+    for (index_t j = row[r] + lane; j < row[r + 1]; j += 32)
+        sum += val[j] * __ldg(x + col[j]);
+    for (int offset = 16; offset; offset >>= 1)
+        sum += __shfl_down_sync(0xffffffffu, sum, offset);
+    if (lane == 0) y[r] = sum;
+}
+
+static bool verifyResults(const double* ref, const double* got, index_t n) {
+    for (index_t i = 0; i < n; ++i) {
+        const double err = std::abs(got[i] - ref[i]);
+        if ((std::abs(ref[i]) < 1e-10 && err > MAX_RELATIVE_ERROR) ||
+            (std::abs(ref[i]) >= 1e-10 && err / std::abs(ref[i]) > MAX_RELATIVE_ERROR)) {
+            std::printf("Validation failed at index %u: reference %.10e, got %.10e\n",
+                        i, ref[i], got[i]);
+            return false;
         }
     }
     return true;
 }

+static void spmvCpu(const double* val, const index_t* col, const index_t* row,
+                    const double* x, index_t nrows, double* y) {
+#pragma omp parallel for schedule(dynamic, 64)
+    for (int64_t i = 0; i < static_cast<int64_t>(nrows); ++i) {
+        double sum = 0.0;
+        for (index_t j = row[i]; j < row[i + 1]; ++j) sum += val[j] * x[col[j]];

+// A warp owns a row.  This is substantially better than one thread per row for
+// the benchmark's long rows and remains efficient for short, irregular rows.
+__global__ void spmvWarpKernel(const double* __restrict__ val,
+                               const index_t* __restrict__ col,
+                               const index_t* __restrict__ row,
+                               const double* __restrict__ x,
+                               index_t nrows, double* __restrict__ y) {
+    const unsigned lane = threadIdx.x & 31u;
+    const index_t r = (blockIdx.x * blockDim.x + threadIdx.x) >> 5;
+    if (r >= nrows) return;
+    double sum = 0.0;
+    for (index_t j = row[r] + lane; j < row[r + 1]; j += 32)
+        sum += val[j] * __ldg(x + col[j]);
+    for (int offset = 16; offset; offset >>= 1)
+        sum += __shfl_down_sync(0xffffffffu, sum, offset);
+    if (lane == 0) y[r] = sum;
+}
+
+static bool verifyResults(const double* ref, const double* got, index_t n) {
+    for (index_t i = 0; i < n; ++i) {
+        const double err = std::abs(got[i] - ref[i]);
+        if ((std::abs(ref[i]) < 1e-10 && err > MAX_RELATIVE_ERROR) ||
+            (std::abs(ref[i]) >= 1e-10 && err / std::abs(ref[i]) > MAX_RELATIVE_ERROR)) {
+            std::printf("Validation failed at index %u: reference %.10e, got %.10e\n",
+                        i, ref[i], got[i]);
+            return false;
         }
     }
     return true;
 }

+static void spmvCpu(const double* val, const index_t* col, const index_t* row,
+                    const double* x, index_t nrows, double* y) {
+#pragma omp parallel for schedule(dynamic, 64)
+    for (int64_t i = 0; i < static_cast<int64_t>(nrows); ++i) {
+        double sum = 0.0;
+        for (index_t j = row[i]; j < row[i + 1]; ++j) sum += val[j] * x[col[j]];
+        y[i] = sum;
     }
 }

-//
-// Purpose:
-//   Verifies correctness of results by comparing to reference solution

-//   reference: array holding the reference result vector
-//   result: array holding the result vector to verify
-//   size: number of elements per vector
-//
-// ****************************************************************************
-bool verifyResults(const double* reference, const double* result, const index_t size) {

-        const double res = result[i];
-        if (std::abs(ref) < 1e-10) {
-            // For very small values, check absolute error
-            if (std::abs(res) > MAX_RELATIVE_ERROR) {
-                printf("Validation failed at index %u: reference %.10e, got %.10e\n", i, ref, res);
-                return false;
-            }
-        } else {
-            // Check relative error
-            const double relError = std::abs((res - ref) / ref);
-            if (relError > MAX_RELATIVE_ERROR) {

-                       i, ref, res, relError);
-                return false;
-            }
+// A warp owns a row.  This is substantially better than one thread per row for
+// the benchmark's long rows and remains efficient for short, irregular rows.
+__global__ void spmvWarpKernel(const double* __restrict__ val,
+                               const index_t* __restrict__ col,
+                               const index_t* __restrict__ row,
+                               const double* __restrict__ x,
+                               index_t nrows, double* __restrict__ y) {
+    const unsigned lane = threadIdx.x & 31u;
+    const index_t r = (blockIdx.x * blockDim.x + threadIdx.x) >> 5;
+    if (r >= nrows) return;
+    double sum = 0.0;
+    for (index_t j = row[r] + lane; j < row[r + 1]; j += 32)
+        sum += val[j] * __ldg(x + col[j]);
+    for (int offset = 16; offset; offset >>= 1)
+        sum += __shfl_down_sync(0xffffffffu, sum, offset);
+    if (lane == 0) y[r] = sum;
+}
+
+static bool verifyResults(const double* ref, const double* got, index_t n) {
+    for (index_t i = 0; i < n; ++i) {
+        const double err = std::abs(got[i] - ref[i]);
+        if ((std::abs(ref[i]) < 1e-10 && err > MAX_RELATIVE_ERROR) ||
+            (std::abs(ref[i]) >= 1e-10 && err / std::abs(ref[i]) > MAX_RELATIVE_ERROR)) {
+            std::printf("Validation failed at index %u: reference %.10e, got %.10e\n",
+                        i, ref[i], got[i]);
+            return false;
         }
     }
     return true;
 }

+static void spmvCpu(const double* val, const index_t* col, const index_t* row,
+                    const double* x, index_t nrows, double* y) {
+#pragma omp parallel for schedule(dynamic, 64)
+    for (int64_t i = 0; i < static_cast<int64_t>(nrows); ++i) {
+        double sum = 0.0;
+        for (index_t j = row[i]; j < row[i + 1]; ++j) sum += val[j] * x[col[j]];
+        y[i] = sum;
     }
 }

-// Function: verifyResults

-bool verifyResults(const double* reference, const double* result, const index_t size) {
-    for (index_t i = 0; i < size; ++i) {

-        const double res = result[i];
-        if (std::abs(ref) < 1e-10) {
-            // For very small values, check absolute error
-            if (std::abs(res) > MAX_RELATIVE_ERROR) {
-                printf("Validation failed at index %u: reference %.10e, got %.10e\n", i, ref, res);
-                return false;
-            }
-        } else {
-            // Check relative error
-            const double relError = std::abs((res - ref) / ref);
-            if (relError > MAX_RELATIVE_ERROR) {
-                printf("Validation failed at index %u: reference %.10e, got %.10e (rel error: %.10e)\n",
-                       i, ref, res, relError);
-                return false;
-            }
+// A warp owns a row.  This is substantially better than one thread per row for
+// the benchmark's long rows and remains efficient for short, irregular rows.
+__global__ void spmvWarpKernel(const double* __restrict__ val,
+                               const index_t* __restrict__ col,
+                               const index_t* __restrict__ row,
+                               const double* __restrict__ x,
+                               index_t nrows, double* __restrict__ y) {
+    const unsigned lane = threadIdx.x & 31u;
+    const index_t r = (blockIdx.x * blockDim.x + threadIdx.x) >> 5;
+    if (r >= nrows) return;
+    double sum = 0.0;
+    for (index_t j = row[r] + lane; j < row[r + 1]; j += 32)
+        sum += val[j] * __ldg(x + col[j]);
+    for (int offset = 16; offset; offset >>= 1)
+        sum += __shfl_down_sync(0xffffffffu, sum, offset);
+    if (lane == 0) y[r] = sum;
+}
+
+static bool verifyResults(const double* ref, const double* got, index_t n) {
+    for (index_t i = 0; i < n; ++i) {
+        const double err = std::abs(got[i] - ref[i]);
+        if ((std::abs(ref[i]) < 1e-10 && err > MAX_RELATIVE_ERROR) ||
+            (std::abs(ref[i]) >= 1e-10 && err / std::abs(ref[i]) > MAX_RELATIVE_ERROR)) {
+            std::printf("Validation failed at index %u: reference %.10e, got %.10e\n",
+                        i, ref[i], got[i]);
+            return false;
         }
     }
     return true;
 }

+static void spmvCpu(const double* val, const index_t* col, const index_t* row,
+                    const double* x, index_t nrows, double* y) {
+#pragma omp parallel for schedule(dynamic, 64)
+    for (int64_t i = 0; i < static_cast<int64_t>(nrows); ++i) {
+        double sum = 0.0;
+        for (index_t j = row[i]; j < row[i + 1]; ++j) sum += val[j] * x[col[j]];
+        y[i] = sum;
     }

+// A warp owns a row.  This is substantially better than one thread per row for
+// the benchmark's long rows and remains efficient for short, irregular rows.
+__global__ void spmvWarpKernel(const double* __restrict__ val,
+                               const index_t* __restrict__ col,
+                               const index_t* __restrict__ row,
+                               const double* __restrict__ x,
+                               index_t nrows, double* __restrict__ y) {
+    const unsigned lane = threadIdx.x & 31u;
+    const index_t r = (blockIdx.x * blockDim.x + threadIdx.x) >> 5;
+    if (r >= nrows) return;
+    double sum = 0.0;
+    for (index_t j = row[r] + lane; j < row[r + 1]; j += 32)
+        sum += val[j] * __ldg(x + col[j]);
+    for (int offset = 16; offset; offset >>= 1)
+        sum += __shfl_down_sync(0xffffffffu, sum, offset);
+    if (lane == 0) y[r] = sum;
+}
+
+static bool verifyResults(const double* ref, const double* got, index_t n) {
+    for (index_t i = 0; i < n; ++i) {
+        const double err = std::abs(got[i] - ref[i]);
+        if ((std::abs(ref[i]) < 1e-10 && err > MAX_RELATIVE_ERROR) ||
+            (std::abs(ref[i]) >= 1e-10 && err / std::abs(ref[i]) > MAX_RELATIVE_ERROR)) {
+            std::printf("Validation failed at index %u: reference %.10e, got %.10e\n",
+                        i, ref[i], got[i]);
+            return false;
         }
     }
     return true;
 }

codex
Implemented the hybrid MPI + OpenMP + CUDA SpMV benchmark.

Key changes:

- MPI partitions CSR rows and nonzeros across ranks.
- Each rank selects a node-local GPU.
- CUDA uses a warp-per-row SpMV kernel.
- GPU memory remains allocated across timed iterations.
- OpenMP parallelizes host reference computation and CSR normalization.
- Results are gathered to rank 0 for output and validation.
- Timing uses the slowest MPI rank for accurate distributed performance.
- CMake now requires and links CUDA, MPI, and OpenMP.
- Executable remains `spmv`; no files were added.

Validation completed successfully with:

- One MPI rank / one GPU.
- Two MPI ranks / two GPUs.
- Four MPI ranks with more ranks than matrix rows.
- OpenMP enabled with two threads per rank.

Modified files: [spmv.cpp](/home/llmtest/evals/20260805-120633/spmv_gpt-5.6-sol-low_hybrid_r3/spmv/spmv.cpp) and [CMakeLists.txt](/home/llmtest/evals/20260805-120633/spmv_gpt-5.6-sol-low_hybrid_r3/spmv/CMakeLists.txt).

+static void spmvCpu(const double* val, const index_t* col, const index_t* row,
+                    const double* x, index_t nrows, double* y) {
+#pragma omp parallel for schedule(dynamic, 64)
+    for (int64_t i = 0; i < static_cast<int64_t>(nrows); ++i) {
+        double sum = 0.0;
+        for (index_t j = row[i]; j < row[i + 1]; ++j) sum += val[j] * x[col[j]];
+        y[i] = sum;
     }
 }
 
-// ****************************************************************************
-// Function: verifyResults
-//
-// Purpose:
-//   Verifies correctness of results by comparing to reference solution
-//
-// Arguments:
-//   reference: array holding the reference result vector
-//   result: array holding the result vector to verify
-//   size: number of elements per vector
-//
-// ****************************************************************************
-bool verifyResults(const double* reference, const double* result, const index_t size) {
-    for (index_t i = 0; i < size; ++i) {
-        const double ref = reference[i];
-        const double res = result[i];
-        if (std::abs(ref) < 1e-10) {

-            if (std::abs(res) > MAX_RELATIVE_ERROR) {
-                printf("Validation failed at index %u: reference %.10e, got %.10e\n", i, ref, res);
-                return false;
-            }
-        } else {
-            // Check relative error
-            const double relError = std::abs((res - ref) / ref);
-            if (relError > MAX_RELATIVE_ERROR) {
-                printf("Validation failed at index %u: reference %.10e, got %.10e (rel error: %.10e)\n",
-                       i, ref, res, relError);
-                return false;
-            }
+// A warp owns a row.  This is substantially better than one thread per row for
+// the benchmark's long rows and remains efficient for short, irregular rows.
+__global__ void spmvWarpKernel(const double* __restrict__ val,
+                               const index_t* __restrict__ col,
+                               const index_t* __restrict__ row,
+                               const double* __restrict__ x,
+                               index_t nrows, double* __restrict__ y) {
+    const unsigned lane = threadIdx.x & 31u;
+    const index_t r = (blockIdx.x * blockDim.x + threadIdx.x) >> 5;
+    if (r >= nrows) return;
+    double sum = 0.0;
+    for (index_t j = row[r] + lane; j < row[r + 1]; j += 32)
+        sum += val[j] * __ldg(x + col[j]);
+    for (int offset = 16; offset; offset >>= 1)
+        sum += __shfl_down_sync(0xffffffffu, sum, offset);
+    if (lane == 0) y[r] = sum;
+}
+
+static bool verifyResults(const double* ref, const double* got, index_t n) {
+    for (index_t i = 0; i < n; ++i) {
+        const double err = std::abs(got[i] - ref[i]);
+        if ((std::abs(ref[i]) < 1e-10 && err > MAX_RELATIVE_ERROR) ||
+            (std::abs(ref[i]) >= 1e-10 && err / std::abs(ref[i]) > MAX_RELATIVE_ERROR)) {
+            std::printf("Validation failed at index %u: reference %.10e, got %.10e\n",
+                        i, ref[i], got[i]);
+            return false;
         }
     }
     return true;
 }

+    // Contiguous row ownership makes collection cheap; CSR payloads are scattered

tokens used
30,097
Implemented the hybrid MPI + OpenMP + CUDA SpMV benchmark.

Key changes:

- MPI partitions CSR rows and nonzeros across ranks.
- Each rank selects a node-local GPU.
- CUDA uses a warp-per-row SpMV kernel.
- GPU memory remains allocated across timed iterations.
- OpenMP parallelizes host reference computation and CSR normalization.
- Results are gathered to rank 0 for output and validation.
- Timing uses the slowest MPI rank for accurate distributed performance.
- CMake now requires and links CUDA, MPI, and OpenMP.
- Executable remains `spmv`; no files were added.

Validation completed successfully with:

- One MPI rank / one GPU.
- Two MPI ranks / two GPUs.
- Four MPI ranks with more ranks than matrix rows.
- OpenMP enabled with two threads per rank.

Modified files: [spmv.cpp](/home/llmtest/evals/20260805-120633/spmv_gpt-5.6-sol-low_hybrid_r3/spmv/spmv.cpp) and [CMakeLists.txt](/home/llmtest/evals/20260805-120633/spmv_gpt-5.6-sol-low_hybrid_r3/spmv/CMakeLists.txt).
